tbmm-2017-12-16sit02spe0135par0181	Mr. President, I have made a commitment to speak for the National Movement Party Group on the budgets of the esteemed MPs, the Ministry of Interior, the Police General Directorate, the General Command of the Police, the Undersecretary of Public Order and Security, the Coast Guard Command and Immigration Administration General Directorate. Dear lawmakers, I thank Mr. Home Secretary, Interior Ministry employees, governors, governors, and all our security forces separately for their decisive efforts to combat terrorism. We, the National Movement Party, believe that your determination to fight terrorism must continue until the last terrorist is eliminated. Let our security forces and the Home Office move on without looking back. The great Turkish nation and the National Movement Party are with them until the end. Our country is under more pressure than ever before in its history. So it's imperative that we throw our inner self into our hearts and place ourselves in our hearts against those who are targeting the survival of our state. That's exactly what the National Movement Party stands for. On the other hand, the threat of terrorism that we're dealing with today has become very versatile, and it's involved in conflicting relationships. So the anti-terrorism strategies that will be implemented should be shaped accordingly, not just limited to the armed struggle of our heroic security forces. In view of other operations and infiltration attempts, terrorists have been observed to have changed their way of infiltrating our country and the target counties have shifted west. There are certain measures that must be taken. Some measures have been taken on border security, especially on our land borders, and the continuation of this must be carried out. First of all, the main issue of the Ministry of Culture and Tourism in Turkey is the engine power of Turkey's national, cultural independence struggle. For two centuries, Turkey has, unfortunately, been struggling for a great cultural independence. Under the influence of other civilizations, other cultures, Turkey struggles to build its own culture, to rise above the power of its national culture, on its national values internationally. In this framework, Turkey's battle for national, cultural independence and the struggle for economic, technological independence actually constitutes two key areas of conflict that have been going simultaneously with each other in the last two centuries, the idea and political struggle. Precious lawmakers, culture and tourism, although both are different by their qualifications, are important areas in which studies in both areas affect and strengthen one another. tbmm-2017-12-16sit04spe0679par0140	Mr. President, the valued MPs are envisioned to be submitted to the authorities for the purpose of informing members of the International Labour Organization's Main Convention, Article 19 of the Convention, the contract and recommendation adopted at the International Study Conference. Last June, I will brief you on the International Labour Organization's decision to work and to work for Peace and Solidarity, which was adopted at the 106/nth International Work Conference, and was voted to be adopted by our country at the end of the conference. The repeat of conflicts and disasters, the global refugee crisis, has increased the need for employment-based intervention worldwide. The June 16, 2017 decision on employment and respectable employment advice for Peace and Solidarity guides member states to prevent crisis situations stemming from conflict and disaster, to ensure improvement, peace and resilience to the crisis, to take measures related to employment and respectable work. This recommendation decision, which has no bindings on member states, which is more of a guide, consists of 49 articles from 14 chapters, except the entry section. In summary, it is advised that member states respect basic principles and rights, other human rights, and relevant international labour force standards at the workplace as they adopt and implement a comprehensive and sustainable employment strategy in the construction of resistance in the wake of conflict or disaster. The recommendation includes employees, job seekers and employers in all sectors of the economy affected by conflicts and disasters. The Member States are asked to provide as quickly as possible basic income assurances after the crisis, to develop comprehensive social security programmes and other social protection mechanisms, focusing on all disadvantaged groups, including immigrants and refugees who have become more vulnerable by the crisis, taking into account national legislation and international agreements. In the case of the refugee flow, which is one of the consequences of the crisis, it is envisioned that the member states should accept the importance of sharing equal burden and responsibility, co-operate with member states in order to strengthen international co-operation and solidarity for the support of less developed or developing countries that accept a large number of refugees, to ensure the access to respectable jobs and livelihood opportunities, to facilitate their security situation in their country. In countries where there are predictable risks related to conflict or disaster, it is stressed that all measures involved in the decision should be developed within the mechanism of social dialogue, such as establishing endurance, preventing crises, reducing crises and describing risks to intervene in the crisis, assessing and emergency intervention. The advice decision encourages member states to strengthen their co-operation with bilateral or multilateral agreements through the United Nations, international financial institutions, other regional and international mechanisms. With respect to the knowledge of the High Council.

We believe that Turkey will be strong if the woman in this area is strong, and we believe that there is a strong element in the process of developing, depending on the synergy of women and young people. Once again in office, during the prime minister's term, we had an action plan to develop women's employment. We have the Women's Employment Watch Board. We've taken steps towards the immediate operation of this board, we've held meetings with all of our concerned NGOs, academics, fellow members, and we've started our work on the revision of our Prime Ministerial Generality. On the other hand, we've had some very serious incentives that have been going on for a very long time for ten years. I can count them one by one, but if I count them, I can't get into the other stuff. For 48 months, the activities of our women with professional qualifications, on the other hand, at the Turkish Employment Institution of our women who have received vocational training and vocational training courses -- also answer the question of Şenal Hanım -- are young and related to women -- are all courses that are guaranteed for employment. By asking for a minimum 50% employment guarantee, we organise these courses, and I have to say that we are getting serious positive results from these courses. And it's clear to the numbers. I have to express that our women's attention and interest in the activities we have been especially involved in by the ISKUR. In the last 12 years, 753,000 women have taken advantage of our vocational courses, a total of 1,405,000; half of them are women. Again, the number of women who benefit from our in-house training programmes is 408,000; again, half of the total, 50 percent. Now, to further increase our rates, we will also provide 400 euros of support for our women who are obliged to take care of two-to-five-year-olds in our employment training programme, our business programmes and vocational training courses, as well as child support for the first time in which our President has announced. With these rates, hopefully, we think women will benefit more from our activities and that their employment participation will eliminate obstacles to entry into the labour market. There are a lot of differences between the partners in trusty flexible working models, but we also have an obligation to offer secure flexible working models, especially in terms of women and young people, as a strong alternative, as a social state, if it is our responsibility as ministry to regulate every area of work, as a policy, as a choice in front of women and young people. After that, after we've established the rules, after we've established these practices, we think that the choice depends on women and young people. In this sense, guaranteed flexible working models are models that are applied around the world; in transitional periods, they can be temporarily preferred, in relation to the processes in their lives. Depending on this periodary period, pensions and pensions will already be resolved. On the other hand, child labor and seasonal agricultural workers have become a subject you've been focusing on.
